Also one does split neither with a hatchet, nor with a sickle, nor with a plane, but with a dagger. From a room full of produce which was blocked but became accessible one takes from the opening. Rebbi Meïr says, also one freshly makes accessible and takes³⁶.

footnotes:
³⁴ Building materials which are not intended to be used as fire wood and therefore are muqṣeh
³⁵ Since the log was whole and therefore muqṣeh
³⁶ He holds that on a holiday one needs food, one may open the storage facility in any way one sees fit.


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 4:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:3:2)


**הלכה:** אֵין מְבַקְּעִין עֵצִים מִן הַקּוֹרוֹת וְלֹא מִן הַקּוֹרָה שֶׁנִּשְׁבְּרָה. הָדָא דְאַתְּ אָמַר. בַּקּוֹרָה שֶׁנִּשְׁבְּרָה בְיוֹם טוֹב. אֲבָל בַּקּוֹרָה שֶׁנִּשְׁבְּרָה מֵעֶרֶב יוֹם טוֹב מוּתָּר בְּשֶׁאֵין בָּהֶן תּוֹאַר כֶּלִי. אֲבָל אִם יֵשׁ עָלֶיהָ תוֹאַר כֶּלִי. אֲפִילוּ נִשְׁבְּרָה מֵעֶרֶב יוֹם טוֹב אָסוּר.


**HALAKHAH:** “One does not split logs nor a log which broke.” That is, a log which broke on the holiday. But a log which broke on the eve of a holiday is permitted if it cannot be called an implement. But if it can be called an implement even if it broke on the eve of a holiday it is forbidden³⁷.

footnotes:
³⁷ Building material which broke before the holiday and cannot be used any more automatically is classified as fuel. Since preparing food on the holiday is permitted, fuel is needed and the broken log is usable on the holiday. But if the broken log still may be used in any way as an implement, it is worth more as an implement than as fuel and may be used as the latter only if it was designated as such before the start of the holiday.


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 4:3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:3:3)


תַּנֵּי. סוֹכוֹת תְּאֵינִים וּבָהֶן תְּאֵנִים. מַכְבֶּדוֹת וּבָהֶן תְּמָרִים שֶׁהִכְנִיסָן לְאוֹכְלִין. אוֹכֵל מֵהֶן בְּיוֹם טוֹב. הִכְנִיסָן לְעֵצִים. אֵין אוֹכֵל מֵהֶן בְּיוֹם טוֹב.


It was stated³⁸: “Branches of a fig tree and on them figs, broomsticks {from a date palm}³⁹ and on them dates, which he collected for food, he eats from them on the holiday. If he collected them as wood⁴⁰, he may not eat from them on the holiday.”

footnotes:
³⁸ Tosephta 4:2.
³⁹ Added from G, not in the Tosephta. Palm branches with open leaves are called “broomsticks” since that is what they always are used for.
⁴⁰ Then in his mind the fruits are not food at the start of the holiday and this status cannot change on the holiday.


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 4:3:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:3:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:3:4)


רַב אָמַר. מַסִּיקִין בְּכֵלִים וְאֵין מַסִּיקִין בְשִׁבְרֵי כֵלִים. הָדָא דְאַתְּ אָמַר. בְּכֵלִים שֶׁנִּשְׁבְּרוּ בְּיוֹם טוֹב. אֲבָל בְּכֵלִים שֶׁנִּשְׁבְּרוּ מֵעֶרֶב יוֹם טוֹב מוּתָּר בְּשֶׁאֵין עֲלֶיהֶן תּוֹאַר כֶּלִי. אֲבָל יֵשׁ עֲלֶיהֶן תוֹאַר כֶּלִי וַאֲפִילוּ שֶׁנִּשְׁבְּרוּ בְיוֹם טוֹב מוּתָּר.


Rav said, one heats with implements, one does not heat with broken pieces of implements⁴¹ (except expensive tools of trade, Mishnah *Šabbat*17:4–5), they can be used for any purpose on the holiday. Broken pieces usually are *muqṣeh*. Babli *Šabbat*124b.. That is, vessels which broke on the holiday. But vessels which broke on the eve of the holiday are permitted if they cannot be called implements⁴². But if they can be called implements even if they broke on the holiday they are permitted⁴³..

footnotes:
⁴¹ Since implements are never muqṣeh
⁴² Same argument as in Note 37.
⁴³ As long as the broken piece is still usable in some way, it is an implement and cannot be muqṣeh


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 4:3:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:3:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:3:5)


תַּנֵּי רִבִּי חִיָיה. מַסִּיקִין בְּאוֹכְלִין. אֲבָל לֹא בִקְלִיפֵּיהֶן וְלֹא בְגַלְעִינֵיהֶן. רַב הֲוָה יְתִיב קוֹמֵי רִבִּי חִיָיה רָבָּה וַהֲוָה אֲכִיל. וַהֲוָה מִתְחַמֵּי זְרֵק גַּו תַּפְיִיהּ וּזְרֵק קוֹמֵי תַפְיֵיהּ. אָמַר לֵיהּ רִבִּי חִיָיה רַבָּה. הָכָא זְרוֹק. אָמַר לֵיהּ. מַה בֵין הָכָא וּמַה בֵין הָכָא. אָמַר לֵיהּ. בֶּן פֵּיחָה. אַחֲרֵיהֶם אַחֲרֵיהֶם. אָמַר רִבִּי חֲנִינָה. אוֹכְלָה הֲוָה זְרֵק. אָמַר רִבִּי אִימִּי בַּבְלָיָיא. חוֹלֶה הֲוָה. אָמַר לֵיהּ. כְּנֶגֶד כֵּן אָסוּר בְּיוֹם טוֹב.


Rebbi Ḥiyya stated: One may heat with edibles, but not with their shells or pits⁴⁴ it may be used in any way one desires. But shells and pits are waste and have the same status as broken logs.. Rav was sitting before the Elder Rebbi Ḥiyya and was eating. He was seen throwing into the stove and in front of the stove⁴⁵. The Elder Rebbi Ḥiyya said to him, throw in this way⁴⁶. He asked him, what is the difference between this way and that way? He said to him, pasha’s son, behind them, behind them⁴⁷. Rebbi Ḥanina said, he was throwing food⁴⁸. The Babylonian Rebbi Immi⁴⁹ said, he was sick⁵⁰. He said to him, in this way it would be forbidden on the holiday⁵¹..

footnotes:
⁴⁴ Since food is not muqṣeh
⁴⁵ The pits and shells of the fruits he ate.
⁴⁶ He required that all waste be thrown into the oven.
⁴⁷ Since food waste is unpleasant it has to be disposed of so it is not seen.
⁴⁸ He only sucked the sap of the fruit and disposed of the dry pulp.
⁴⁹ He is R. Immi mentioned elsewhere without the predicate “Babylonian”.
⁵⁰ And therefore could use the fruit only partially what for a healthy person would have been prohibited waste.
⁵¹ Even if some pulp was left on the fruit, since it could be used neither by the eater nor by any other person its status on the holiday would not be different from shells and pits. The story is an illustration of R. Ḥiyya’s baraita


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 4:3:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:3:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:3:6)


וְלֹא בְּקוֹרְדוֹם. דְּבֵית רִבִּי יַנַּאי אָמְרִין. מְבַקְּעִין בְּקוֹרְדוֹם. תַּנָּא רִבִּי הוֹשַׁעְיָה. מְבַקְּעִין בְּקֹרְדוֹם. שְׁמוּאֵל אָמַר. הֲלָכָה כְמִי שֶׁהוּא אוֹמֵר. מְבַקְּעִין בְּקֹרְדוֹם. תַּנֵּי. רִבִּי יוֹסֵי בֶּן כִּופֵּר אָמַר מִשֵּׁם רִבִּי לָעְזָר בֶּן שַׁמּוּעַ. בֵּית שַׁמַּי אוֹסְרִין וּבֵית הִלֵּל מַתִּירִין.


“Neither with a hatchet.” In the House of Rebbi Yannai they are saying, one splits with a hatchet⁵². Rebbi Hoshaia stated, one splits with a hatchet. Samuel said, practice follows him who said, one splits with a hatchet. It was stated: Rebbi Yose ben Kipper said in the name of Rebbi Eleazar ben Shamua, the House of Shammai forbid but the House of Hillel permit.

footnotes:
⁵² Here practice is decided against the Mishnah. In the Babli 31b using a hatchet is only permitted if it is done in an unprofessional way.


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 4:3:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:3:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:3:7)


וְלֹא בַמַּגָּל. אִית תַּנָּיֵי תַנֵּי. מְבַקְּעִין. [וְאִית תַּנָּיֵי תַנֵּי. אֵין מְבַקְּעִין. אָמַר רַב חִסְדָּא]. מָאן דְּאָמַר מְבַקְּעִין. בְּמַגָּל שֶׁלְיָד. מָאן דְּאָמַר אֵין מְבַקְּעִין. בְּמַגָּל שֶׁלְקָצִיר.


“Nor with a sickle.” There are Tannaim who state that one splits, and [there are Tannaim who state that one does not split. Rav Ḥisda said,]²⁹ he who said one splits, with a hand-held sickle; he who says one does not split, with a scythe⁵³.

footnotes:
²⁹ Corrector’s addition, confirmed by G.
⁵³ Any tool which also is used by the non-professional may be used, but not a tool only used by professionals.


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 4:3:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:3:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:3:8)


אִית תַּנָּיֵי תַנֵּי. מְפַסְפְּסִין בִּקְלָקִים. אִית תַּנָּיֵי תַנֵּי. אֵין מְפַסְפְּסִין. אָמַר רַב חִסְדָּא. מָאן דְּאָמַר. מְפַסְפְּסִין. בְּבָרִיא. מָאן דְּאָמַר. אִין מְפַסְפְּסִין. בְּתָשׁ.


⁵⁴(*Nazir*6:3 Note 169). One of the obligations of the *nazir* is to let his hair grow; he is forbidden to tear out a single hair. Therefore it is a question whether he may rub his hair with coarse cloth (in this case Cilician, κιλίκιον). It is permitted only for persons with healthy hair. There are Tannaim who state that one separates with Cilician cloth, and there are Tannaim who state that one does not separate. Rav Ḥisda said, he who said one separates, for healthy {hair}; he who said one does not separate, for weak {hair}.

footnotes:
⁵⁴ This paragraph, the only one in the present series, does not refer to the rules of holidays but to those of a nazir


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 4:3:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:3:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:3:9)


אִית תַּנָּיֵי תַנֵּי. מוֹלְלִין עֲצֵי בְשָׂמִים. אִית תַּנָּיֵי תַנֵּי. אֵין מוֹלְלִין.‏ מָאן דְּאָמַר. מוֹלְלִין. בְּלַחִים. מָאן דְּאָמַר. אֵין מוֹלְלִין. בִּיבֵישִׁין.


There are Tannaim who state that one rubs aromatic wood⁵⁵; there are Tannaim who state that one does not rub. He who said one rubs, moist ones; he who said one does not rub, dry ones.

footnotes:
⁵⁵ Aromatic wood is not food; therefore it may not be processed on a holiday. But if it is possible to produce perfume simply by rubbing aromatic wood in one’s hand without applying pressure, it is permitted. Babli 33b.


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 4:3:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:3:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:3:10)


אִית תַּנָּיֵי תַנֵּי. מַמְתִּקִין אֶת הַחַרְדָּל בַּגַּחֶלֶת. אִית תַּנָּיֵי תַנֵּי. אֵין מַמְתִּקִין. מָאן דְּאָמַר. מַמְתִּקִין. בְּשֶׁנְּתָנוֹ עַל גַּבֵּי הַגַּחֶלֶת. וּמָאן דְּאָמַר. אֵין מַמְתִּקִין. בְּשֶׁנָּתַן אֶת הַגַּחֶלֶת עָלָיו.


There are Tannaim who state that one sweetens mustard seed on glowing coals; there are Tannaim who state that one does not sweeten. He who said one sweetens, if he puts it on the coals; he who said one does not sweeten, if he puts the coals on it⁵⁶134a but the explanation given there should not be applied to the statement here. It seems that putting coals on mustard seed will extinguish the coals while burning the mustard seed, not sweetening it..

footnotes:
⁵⁶ A similar statement is in the Babli Šabbat


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 4:3:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:3:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:3:11)


אִית תַּנָּיֵי תַנֵּי. שָׁפִין אֶת הַכֶּסֶף בְּקְרֶטִיקוֹן. אִית תַּנָּיֵי תַנֵּי. אֵין שָׁפִין. מָאן דְּאָמַר. שָׁפִין. בְּשֶׁנְּתָנוֹ מֵעֶרֶב יוֹם טוֹב. וּמָאן דְּאָמַר. אֵין שָׁפִין. בְּשֶׁנְּתָנוֹ בְיוֹם טוֹב.


There are Tannaim who state that one rubs silver with chalk⁵⁷; there are Tannaim who state that one does not rub. He who said one rubs, if he puts it on before the start of the holiday; he who said one does not rub, if he puts it on during the holiday.

footnotes:
⁵⁷ This is the modern interpretation starting with Musaphia (Greek κρητυκόν). The Geonic interpretation is “potash”.


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 4:3:12

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:3:12](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:3:12)


רִבִּי מֵאִיר אוֹמֵר אַף הוּא פּוֹחֵת כַּתְּחִילָּה וְנוֹטֵל. רִבִּי בָּא רַב יְהוּדָה בְשֵׁם שְׁמוּאֵל. וּבִלְבַד כְּנֶגֶד הַפֶּתַח. וְהָא תַנֵּי. מוֹדִין חֲכָמִין לְרִבִּי מֵאִיר בְּחוֹתָמוֹת שֶׁבַּקַּרְקַע שֶׁמְפַקְפְּקִין וּמַפְקִיעִין וּמַתִּירִין וְחוֹתְכִין. בַּשַּׁבָּת מְפַקְפְּקִין. אֲבָל לֹא מַפְקִיעִין וְלֹא מַתִּירִין וְלֹא חוֹתְכִין. וּבַכֵּלִים בַּשַּׁבָּת מוּתָּר. וְאֵין צוֹרֶךְ לוֹמַר בְּיוֹם טוֹב.


“Rebbi Meïr says, also one freshly makes accessible and takes³⁶.” Rebbi Abba, Rav Jehudah in the name of Samuel: But only at the place of access⁵⁸. And so it was stated⁵⁹ 15 (Note 22), 20 (Note 66), *Megillah*1:8. Babli 31b. “The Sages agree with Rebbi Meïr about cachets in the ground that one pushes aside, and removes, and unties, and cuts. On the Sabbath one pushes aside but one does not remove nor untie nor cut⁶⁰. For implements on the Sabbath it is permitted⁶¹; it is not necessary to mention on a holiday.”

footnotes:
³⁶ He holds that on a holiday one needs food, one may open the storage facility in any way one sees fit.
⁵⁸ Still one is obligated to minimize the amount of work expended for providing the food.
⁵⁹ Tosephta 3:12; Yerushalmi 5:2, Šabbat
⁶⁰ If produce is stored in the ground and the door tied by a rope, the knot (“cachet”) is not permanent since produce is stored to be used eventually. If on a holiday one needs food, one may open the storage facility in any way one sees fit. In a similar case on the Sabbath one may push the rope to open a cleft through which one can take out some produce but rabbinically one may not otherwise remove the knot even though it is not permanent and removing it from an installation in the ground is not building or tearing down by biblical standards.
⁶¹ Since the notions of building and tearing down buildings does not apply to implements by biblical standards, there is no place for rabbinic restrictions.



### Halakhah 4

###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 4:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:4:1)


**משנה:** אֵין פּוֹתְחִין אֶת הַנֵּר מִפְּנֵי שֶׁהוּא עוֹשֶׂה כֶלִי וְאֵין עוֹשִׂין פֶּיחָמִין וְאֵין חוֹתְכִין אֶת הַפְּתִילָה לִשְׁנָיִם. רִבִּי יְהוּדָה אוֹמֵר חוֹתְכָהּ בָּאוּר לִשְׁתֵּי נֵירוֹת:


**MISHNAH:** One does not open the candle⁶²7:4). Since making fire is necessary to prepare food and therefore permitted, for the House of Hillel it is permitted on the holiday for any legitimate reason. because he would make it a vessel nor does one make charcoal⁶³, and one does not cut a wick into two. Rebbi Jehudah says, one may cut it in the fire for two candles⁶⁴.

footnotes:
⁶² This candle is a clay vessel to be put on the top of a candelabrum. It consists of a container for oil and a neck through which the wick is drawn to burn at a safe distance from the oil. If it is new and the opening for the wick is not excavated it cannot be made to work on the holiday since this would be “hitting with hammer”, the formal end of a production process, and therefore a biblical violation both on the holiday and the Sabbath (Mishnah Šabbat
⁶³ Not even for use on the same day (Tosephta 3:14) since preparation of fuel is not included in the permission to use fuel.
⁶⁴ As explained in the Halakhah one may take a long wick, put each end into such a candle and light then in the middle. Then this one action creates two separate burning candles.


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 4:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:4:2)


**הלכה:** תַּנִּי. לָפַּסִּים סְתוּמוֹת. עֲשָׂאָן מֵעֶרֶב יוֹם טוֹב. לֹא יִפְתַּחֵם בְּיוֹם טוֹב. מִפְּנֵי שֶׁהוּא כְּמַכְשִׁיר כֶּלִי בְּיוֹם טוֹב.


**HALAKHAH:** It was stated: Closed pans⁶⁶, if he made them before the holiday he may not open them on the holiday since he would be like one who makes a vessel usable on the holiday⁶⁷, ed. Liebermann 16:13, in Liebermann’s opinion referring only to holiday) it is reported that Rabban Simeon ben Gamliel permits to separate the cover from the pan. R. Ḥananel (*Besah*32a, 5 lines from bottom) and *Sefer Ha`iṭtur*(vol. 2, fol. 150a) read this also in the Yerushalmi text. The statement must have fallen out of our text by scribal error since in the next paragraph a minority opinion in this *baraita* is mentioned, referring to Rabban Simeon ben Gamliel’s opinion, who holds that the pan with its cover form a completed vessel and the separation of cover from pan is to be compared to opening a tightly closed door (explanation of S. Liebermann.).

footnotes:
⁶⁶ Greek λοπάς “pan, plate”.
⁶⁷ The pan is a flat vessel made of clay. It is made together with its cover. It is not fired in a kiln but it is expected to become a permanent vessel by being used over the fire in the kitchen. Before it is used it is completely closed, the cover being connected to the pan by a very thin strip of clay which can be easily cut with a kitchen knife. For this Tanna, cutting the strip is a prime example of “hitting with hammer” and forbidden both on the holiday and on the Sabbath. In the slightly differently worded Tosephta 3:13 (and Tosephta Šabbat


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 4:4:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:4:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:4:3)


תַּמָּן תַּנִּינָן. עַל לָפַּסִּים אִרוֹנִיּוֹת שֶׁהֵן טְהוֹרוֹת בְּאֹהֶל הַמֵּת וּטְמֵאוֹת בְּמַשָּׂא הַזָּב. רִבִּי לָעְזָר בֵּירִבִּי צָדוֹק אוֹמֵר. אַף בְּמַשָּׂא הַזָּב טְהוֹרוֹת. מִפְּנֵי שֶׁלֹּא נִגְמְרָה מְלַאכְתָּן. אַתְיָא דִיחִידָאָה דְהָכָא כִּסְתוּמָה דְתַמָּן. וְדִיחִידָיָיא דְתַמָּן כִּסְתוּמָה דְהָכָא.


There, we have stated⁶⁸2:5, missing the introduction, that R. Ismael did not indicate his position regarding the difference between the anonymous Tanna and R. Eleazar ben R. Ṣadoq.: “About rural pans⁶⁹, that they are pure in a tent with a corpse⁷⁰2:7). Since the unused rural pan is totally closed, it is immune to the impurity of the dead by biblical decree, *Num*. 19:15. but impure if carried by a person afflicted with gonorrhea⁷¹ 15:10, even if the sufferer never touches the implement and moves it on a flat piece of wood (or clay) impervious to impurity.. Rebbi Eleazar ben Rebbi Ṣadoq says, even if carried by a person afflicted with gonorrhea they are pure since their production was not finished⁷².” It turns out that the minority opinion here corresponds to the majority opinion there and the minority opinion there corresponds to the majority opinion here⁷³.

footnotes:
⁶⁸ Mishnah Idiut
⁶⁹ Pans made of unfired clay with cover attached.
⁷⁰ A clay vessel cannot become impure from the outside. A clay vessel which does not have a cavity is immune to impurity (Mishnah Kelim
⁷¹ Any implement moved by a sufferer from gonorrhea is severely impure, Lev.
⁷² He holds that as long as the cover is not separated from the pan the manufacturing process is not finished and therefore the composite is not an implement to which the rules of impurity may apply.
⁷³ R. Eleazar ben R. Sadoq must agree that separating cover from pot is “hitting with hammer”. Rabban Simeon ben Gamliel must agree that the unopened clay vessel is an implement and subject to impurity by being moved.


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 4:4:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:4:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:4:4)


רִבִּי בָּא בְשֵׁם רַב יְהוּדָה. בְּקַעַת סוֹמְכָהּ לַכּוֹתֶל בִּשְׁבִיל שֶׁלֹּא תְכַסְכֵּס. תַּנֵּי. אֵין מְכַבִּין אֶת הַבְּקַעַת בִּשְׁבִיל לָחוּס עָלֶיהָ. אִם בִּשְׁבִיל שֶׁלֹּא תָעַשֵּׁן אֶת הַבַּיִת וְאֶת הַקְּדֵירָה. מוּתָּר. אָמַר רִבִּי חֲנַנְיָה. בְּשֶׁאֵין שָׁם אֲוִיר. אֲבָל אִם יֵשׁ שָׁם אֲוִיר מַשְׁלִיכָהּ לָאֲוִיר וְדַייוֹ. אָמַר רִבִּי נָסָא. וְיֵאוּת. כֵּן אֲנָן אָמְרִין. הָדָא קִלוּרִיתָה שָׁרֵי מִישׁוֹף וּמִיכְחוֹל מִינָהּ בְּיוֹמָא טָבָא. אָמַר רִבִּי מָנָא. כָּאן לְשִׁימּוּר וְכָאן לִרְפוּאָה.


Rebbi Abba in the name of Rav Jehudah: A splinter one leans on the wall lest it scratches⁷⁴.. It was stated: One does not extinguish a splinter to save it; if to prevent it filling the house or the pot with smoke it is permitted⁷⁵. Rebbi Ḥanania said, if there is no empty space⁷⁶ there. But if there is empty space there one throws it into the empty space and this is enough⁷⁷. Rebbi Nasa said, is this correct? Are we saying so, salve⁷⁸ one is permitted to rub on to use kohl on the holiday⁷⁹? Rebbi Mana said, one is to guard, the other for healing⁸⁰.

footnotes:
⁷⁴ If a splinter of wood constitutes a danger it can be moved even though it is muqṣeh
⁷⁵ If the splinter is burning and represents a nuisance or a danger it may be extinguished even though in general extinguishing a fire is forbidden on the holiday (unless needed in a cooking process). Babli 22a.
⁷⁶ Greek ʼάηρ.
⁷⁷ If the fire can safely be left to burn out by itself, extinguishing it is forbidden.
⁷⁸ Greek κολλύριον.
⁷⁹ Since this is forbidden on the Sabbath and is not connected with food it must be forbidden on the holiday.
⁸⁰ The argument should not even have started. Since making a fire is required in the preparation of food, measures to guard against the dangers of fire must be permitted. But medical procedures other than those necessary to save lives are prohibited on Sabbath and holidays. Babli 22a.


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 4:4:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:4:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:4:5)


שֶׁלֹשָׁה דְּבָרִים נֶאֶמְרוּ בִפְתִילָה לְהַחֲמִיר וּשְׁלֹשָׁה לְהָקֵל. אֵילּוּ שְׁלֹשָׁה שְׁאָמְרוּ לְהַחֲמִיר. אֵין עוֹשִׂין אוֹתָהּ כַּתְּחִילָּה בְיוֹם טוֹב. וְאֵין חוֹתְכִין אוֹתָהּ לִשְׁנַיִם. וְאִין מְסַפְסְפִין אוֹתָהּ בָּאוּר. אֵילּוּ שְׁלֹשָׁה שְׁאָמְרוּ לְהָקֵל. מוֹעֲכִין אוֹתָהּ וְשׁוֹרִין אוֹתָהּ בַּשֶּׁמֶן. וְחוֹלְקִין אוֹתָהּ בָּאוּר לִשְׁתֵּי נֵרוֹת. אָמַר רִבִּי חִינְנָא. בְּצָרִיךְ לִשְׁנֵיהֶן. תַּנֵּי. רִבִּי יוּדָה בַּר פָּזִי דְּבַר דְּלָיָיא. מֵבִיא שְׁתֵּי נֵרוֹת ונוֹתְנָהּ בִּשְׁנֵיהֶן וְחוֹלְקָהּ בָּאוּר לִשְׁנֵי נֵירוֹת. רִבִּי יַעֲקֹב בַּר אָחָא חִינְנָא בַּר אִידִי בְשֵׁם רַב. מוֹהַטִין אֶת הַפְּתִילָה בְיוֹם טוֹב.


⁸¹“About a wick three things were said as restrictions and three as leniencies. The following three were said as restrictions: One does not make it new on the holiday, nor does one cut one into two pieces, nor does one singe it by fire⁸². The following three were said as leniencies: One softens it⁸³, and soaks it in oil, and divides it into two lights by fire.” Rebbi Ḥinena said, if he needs both of them. It was stated, Rebbi Jehudah bar Pazi in the name of Bar Delaia: He brings two candles, introduces it into both of them, and separates them by fire into two candles⁶⁴. Rebbi Jacob bar Aḥa, Ḥinena bar Idi in the name of Rav: One cleans the wick⁸⁴ on a holiday.

footnotes:
⁸¹ Tosephta 3:21, Babli 32b.
⁸² To make it easier to be lit.
⁸³ By working it with his fingers.
⁶⁴ As explained in the Halakhah one may take a long wick, put each end into such a candle and light then in the middle. Then this one action creates two separate burning candles.
⁸⁴ Cutting off the burned-out part.



### Halakhah 5

###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 4:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:5:1)


**משנה:** אֵין שׁוֹבְרִין אֶת הַחֶרֶס וְאֵין חוֹתְכִין אֶת הַנְּיָיר לִצְלוֹת בּוֹ מָלִיחַ. אֵין גּוֹרְפִין תַּנּוּר וְכִירַיִים אֲבָל מְכַבְּשִׁין. אֵין מַקִּיפִין שְׁתֵּי חָבִיּוֹת לִשְׁפּוֹת עֲלֵיהֶן אֶת הַקְּדֵירָה. אֵין סוֹמְכִין אֶת הַקְּדֵירָה בַבַּקַּעַת. וְכֵן הַדֶּלֶת. אֵין מַנְהִיגִין אֶת הַבְּהֵמָה בַמַּקֵּל בְּיוֹם טוֹב.


**MISHNAH:** One does not break earthenware nor cut paper to grill on them salted fish⁸⁵. One does not clean out stove or cooking range but one smooths⁸⁶. One does not arrange two amphoras to cook a pot on them⁸⁷. One does not support a pot by a splinter, nor the door⁸⁸. One does not drive an animal with a stick on the holiday⁸⁹.

footnotes:
⁸⁵ One does not break a potsherd in two to use part of it on a grill to protect the fish from being burned by the irons of the grill. According to Maimonides, for the same purpose one uses a special kind of paper impregnated with oil which will not burn as long as it is still moist.
⁸⁶ One may not clean out all ashes from a stove but one may smooth the surface of the ashes to have a level place to put in new wood or charcoal.
⁸⁷ If one does not have a stove, one may not on the holiday create one by placing two amphoras side by side with space between them for fire wood and then put the pot between them to be cooked by the fire started between the amphoras.
⁸⁸ This statement is valid only for those who hold that pieces of wood are prepared only to be fuel but nothing else.
⁸⁹ Since this is herders’ practice on working days.


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 4:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:5:2)


**הלכה:** הָדָא אָמְרָה. הָהֵן סִיקִירָה אֲסִיר מֵיעֲבֲד בְּיוֹמָא טָבָא. דְּהוּא מַקְטַע בְּגוּמֵיהּ.


**HALAKHAH:** The sausage maker cannot do it on the holiday since he is cutting bast⁹⁰7, Note 292..

footnotes:
⁹⁰ Šabbat


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 4:5:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:5:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:5:3)


בְּרַתֵּיהּ דְּרִבִּי חִיָיה רַבָּה אֲתַת מֵיפָה גַּו תַּנּוּרָא. אַשְׁכַּחַת כִּיפָּה בַגַוֵּיהּ. אֲתַת שְׁאָלַת לַאֲבוּהָא. אֲמַר לָהּ. אִיזְלִין גָּרְפִין. אָמְרָה. לֵינָה יָכְלָה. אֲמַר לָהּ. אִיזְלִין כָּבְשִׁין. יָדְעָה הֲוָות. אֶלָּא דַהֲוָות בְּעָיָא מִישְׁמַע מִן אָבוּהּ.


⁹¹ The daughter of the Elder Rebbi Ḥiyya went to bake in the stove. She found a heap in it. She came and asked her father who said to her, go and empty it. She said, I cannot do it⁹². He said to her, go and smoothen it. She had known this⁹³ but she wanted to hear it from her father⁹⁴

footnotes:
⁹¹ Babli 32b, reported about his wife (who did not have the scholarly reputation of her daughter.)
⁹² Since it is forbidden in the Mishnah.
⁹³ If she knew the Mishnah, she must have known that smoothing the ashes is permitted.
⁹⁴ Whether he had any modifications of the Mishnah.


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 4:5:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:5:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:5:4)


רַב אָמַר. מַצִּילִין מִן הַתַּנּוּר לַכִּירָה. אֲבָל לֹא מִכִּירָה לַתַּנּוּר. רַב אַבָּא בַּר חָנָה אָמַר. מַצִּילִין מִתַּנּוּר לַכִּירָה וּמִכִּירָה לַתַּנּוּר. אֲבָל לֹא מֵעַרְבִית לַשַּׁחֲרִית. רַב אָבוּן בְּשֵׁם רַב. מַצִּילִין מִתַּנּוּר לַכִּירָה וּמִכִּירָה לַתַּנּוּר. מֵעַרְבִית לַשַּׁחֲרִית אֲבָל לֹא מִיּוֹם טוֹב לַחוֹל.


Rav said, one transfers from oven to stove but not from stove to oven⁹⁵. Rav Abba bar Ḥana⁹⁶ said, one transfers from oven to stove and from stove to oven but not from evening to morning. Rav Avun in the name of Rav: One transfers from oven to stove, from stove to oven, and from evening to morning, but not from holiday to weekday⁹⁷.

footnotes:
⁹⁵ The oven is a clay vessel in the form of a truncated cone. In the draft generated from the fire, the temperature is higher than that on top of an open stove. One may transfer completely cooked meals on a lesser fire to keep them hot. No reason is given why the inverse transfer is not permitted.
⁹⁶ Rav’s second cousin. The entire discussion is about their family’s common usage.
⁹⁷ It is generally accepted that the permission (or obligation) to cook on the holiday refers only to holiday food. It is forbidden to intentionally cook on the holiday for a following weekday.


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 4:5:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:5:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:5:5)


אֵין מַקִּיפִין שְׁתֵּי חָבִיּוֹת לִשְׁפּוֹת עֲלֵיהֶן אֶת הַקְּדֵירָה. מִשֵּׁם בּוֹנֶה. תַּנֵּי. לֹא יֵצֵא סוּמֵא בְמַקְלוֹ וְלֹא רוֹעֶה בְתַרְמִילוֹ. וְרִבִּי שִׁמְעוֹן מַתִּיר. וְכֵן הָיָה רִבִּי שִׁמְעוֹן אוֹמֵר. הוֹאִיל וְהוּא מוּתָּר בְּדָבָר אֶחָד. יְהֵא מוּתָּר בְּכָל הַדְּבָרִים.


“One does not arrange two amphoras to cook a pot on them.” Because of building⁹⁸. It was stated⁹⁹: “A blind person may not go out with his stick, nor the shepherd with his bag, but Rebbi Simeon permits.” So was Rebbi Simeon saying: Since one thing is permitted to him, let him be permitted everything¹⁰⁰.

footnotes:
⁹⁸ Not building a permanent structure but fabricating an implement, violating “hitting with hammer”.
⁹⁹ Tosephta 3:17, Babli 25b.
¹⁰⁰ The anonymous author forbids to the blind and the shepherd to go in public the same way they do on weekdays. R. Simeon objects since the blind man and the shepherd can freely move their implements in the house; there is no reason to forbid the same in public.



### Halakhah 6

###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 4:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:6:1)


**משנה:** רִבִּי אֱלִיעֶזֶר אוֹמֵר נוֹטֵל אָדָם קִיסָם לַחֲצוֹץ בּוֹ שִׁנָּיו וּמְגַבֵּב מִן הֶחָצֵר וּמַדְלִיק שֶׁכָּל מַה שֶּׁבֶּחָצֵר מוּכָן. וַחֲכָמִים אוֹמְרִים מְגַבֵּב מִשֶּׁלְּפָנָיו וּמַדְלִיק:


**MISHNAH:** Rebbi Eliezer says, a person takes a chip to use as a toothpick¹⁰¹ and collects from the courtyard and ignites¹⁰² since anything in the courtyard is prepared, but the Sages are saying one collects from what is in front of him¹⁰³ but they do not authorize sweeping large areas to collect them. Babli 33b. and ignites.

footnotes:
¹⁰¹ Even though the chip either was intended as fuel or was regarded as waste. One may not take a chip from any wood connected to the ground.
¹⁰² Uses as firewood to start a fire. R. Eliezer permits to sweep the entire courtyard to collect the little pieces of wood which are useful to start fires.
¹⁰³ They agree that slivers of wood in house or courtyard are never muqṣeh


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 4:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:6:2)


**הלכה:** מַה בֵינֵיהֶן. לַעֲשׂוֹת צִיבּוּר בֵּינֵיהֶן. עַל דַּעְתֵּיהּ דְּרִבִּי אֱלִיעֶזֶר. עוֹשִׂין צִיבּוּר. עַל דַּעְתֵּהוֹן דְּרַבָּנִן. אֵין עוֹשִׂין צִיבּוּר.


**HALAKHAH:** What is between them? To make a heap. In Rebbi Eliezer’s opinion one may make a heap, in the rabbis’ opinion one may not make a heap.



### Halakhah 7

###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 4:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:7:1)


**משנה:** אֵין מוֹצִיאִין אֶת הָאוּר לֹא מִן הָעֵצִים וְלֹא מִן הָאֲבָנִים וְלֹא מִן הַמַּיִם. וְאֵין מְלַבְּנִין אֶת הָרְעָפִים לִצְלוֹת עֲלֵיהֶן. וְעוֹד אָמַר רִבִּי אֱלִיעֶזֶר עוֹמֵד אָדָם עַל הַמּוּקְצֶה עֶרֶב שַׁבָּת בַּשְּׁבִיעִית וְאוֹמֵר מִיכָּן אֲנִי אוֹכֵל לְמָחָר. וַחֲכָמִים אוֹמְרִים עַד שֶׁיִּרְשׁוֹם וְיֹאמַר מִיכָּן וְעַד כָּאן:


**MISHNAH:** One makes fire neither with stones¹⁰⁶, nor with wood¹⁰⁷, nor with water¹⁰⁸, and one does not heat tiles to fry on them. In addition, Rebbi Eliezer says, a person stands by the *Muqṣeh*¹⁰⁹ on a Sabbath eve in a sabbatical year¹¹⁰ and says, from here I shall eat tomorrow, but the Sages say, only if he delineates and says, from here to here.

footnotes:
¹⁰⁶ One may not generate sparks by hitting flintstones one on the other or on a metal surface. It could have said that one does not start a new fire in any way since this is not included in the permission to prepare food.
¹⁰⁷ By turning a wooden stick rapidly in a hole in a wooden log.
¹⁰⁸ By filling a round glass vessel with water and using it as a magnifying glass concentrating the sun’s rays to start a fire.
¹⁰⁹ The place on the roof where fig cakes are spread out to ferment and dry. This presumes that there are parts which already are edible again.
¹¹⁰ When there are no tithes. The same holds on every Sabbath in a common year if one tithes the drying figs even before they become subject to obligatory tithe. Since food for the Sabbath has to be prepared in advance it cannot be consumed on the Sabbath as a snack for which the rules of tithe do not apply.


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 4:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:7:2)


**הלכה:** אֵין מְלַבְּנִין אֶת הָרְעָפִים לִצְלוֹת עֲלֵיהוֹן. אִית תַּנָּיֵי תַנֵּי. מְלַבְּנִין. [וְאִית תַּנָּיֵי תַנֵּי. אֵין מְלַבְּנִין.] אָמַר רַב חִסְדָּא. מָאן דְּאָמַר. מְלַבְּנִין. בִּבְדוּקִים. וּמָאן דְּאָמַר. אֵין מְלַבְּנִין. בְּשֶׁאֵינָן בְּדוּקִים.


**HALAKHAH:** “One does not heat tiles to fry on them.” There are Tannaim who state, one may heat. [And there are Tannaim who state, one may not heat.]¹¹¹ Rav Ḥisda said, he who said one may heat, by those already checked out. And he who said one may not heat, by those not checked out¹¹².

footnotes:
¹¹¹ Addition by the corrector, not absolutely necessary.
¹¹² The tiles are roof tiles. If they are new and not hardened in a kiln they will be hardened by the procedure which is a prohibited side effect. Since the effect is automatic, the procedure is forbidden even for R. Simeon who forbids procedures only if the Sabbath or holiday infraction is intended. Babli 33b/34a.


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 4:7:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:7:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/4:7:3)


וְעוֹד אָמַר רִבִּי אֱלִיעֶזֶר עוֹמֵד אָדָם עַל הַמּוּקְצֶה עֶרֶב שַׁבָּת בַּשְּׁבִיעִית וְאוֹמֵר מִיכָּן אֲנִי אוֹכֵל לְמָחָר. מִחְלְפָה שִׁיטָּתֵיהּ דְּרִבִּי לִיעֶזֶר. דְּתַנַּינָן תַּמָּן. בֵּית שַׁמַּי אוֹמְרִים. לֹא יִטּוֹל אֶלָּא אִם כֵּן נִעְנַע מִבְּעוֹד יוֹם. וְרִבִּי לִיעֶזֶר לָאו שַׁמֻּתִי הוּא. חוֹמֶר הוּא בַדָּבָר שֶׁיֵּשׁ בּוֹ רוּחַ חַיִים. וַחֲכָמִים אוֹמְרִים עַד שֶׁיִּרְשׁוֹם וְיֹאמַר מִיכָּן וְעַד כָּאן. מִחְלְפָה שִׁיטַּתּוֹן דְּרַבָּנִן. דְּתַנִּינָן תַּמָּן. בֵּית הִלֵּל אוֹמְרִים. עוֹמֵד וְאוֹמֵר. זֶה וָזֶה אֲנִי נוֹטֵל. וְהָכָא אִינּוּן אָמְרִין הָכֵין. עוֹד אִינּוּן אִית לָהוֹן. חוֹמֶר בַּדָּבָר שֶׁיֵּשׁ בּוֹ רוּחַ חַיִים.


¹¹³“In addition, Rebbi Eliezer says, a person stands by the *Muqṣeh* on a Sabbath eve in a sabbatical year and says, from here I shall eat tomorrow.” The argument of Rebbi Eliezer seems inverted, as we have stated there¹¹⁴, “the House of Shammai say, one may not take unless one had moved when it was still daylight,” and is Rebbi Eliezer not a Shammaite¹¹⁵? One is restrictive if living things are involved. “But the Sages say, only if he delineates and says, from here to here.” The argument of the Sages seems inverted, since we have stated there¹¹⁴ “the House of Hillel say, he may stand there and say, this and that I shall take,” and here they are saying so? Still they hold that one is restrictive if living things are involved.

footnotes:
¹¹³ This paragraph is a reformulation of a passage in Halakhah 1:3, Notes 128–138.
¹¹⁴ Mishnah 1:3.
¹¹⁵ This is a very common argument but one may question whether a student of the Hillelite Rabban Joḥanan ben Zakkai can be called a Shammaite even if he was sympathetic to Shammaite rules.
¹¹⁴ Mishnah 1:3.



## Chapter 5


### Halakhah 1

###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 5:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:1:1)


**משנה:** מַשִּׁילִין פֵּירוֹת דֶּרֶךְ אֲרוּבָּה בְּיוֹם טוֹב. וּמְכַסִּין אֶת הַפֵּירוֹת בַּכֵּלִים מִפְּנֵי הַדֶּלֶף. וְכֵן כַּדֵּי יַיִן וְכֵן כַּדֵּי שֶׁמֶן. וְנוֹתְנִין כְּלִי תַּחַת הַדֶּלֶף בַּשַּׁבָּת:


**MISHNAH:** One lowers produce through a skylight¹ on the holiday. Also one covers produce by vessels because of leaks; the same holds for jugs of wine and jugs of oil. On the Sabbath one may put a vessel under a leak².

footnotes:
¹ If there is danger of rain one may remove the produce stored on the flat roof through the skylight.
² To collect the rain water leaking through the roof.


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 5:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:1:2)


**הלכה:** מַשִּׁילִין פֵּירוֹת דֶּרֶךְ אֲרוּבָּה בְּיוֹם טוֹב כול׳. מַתְנִיתָה בְאוֹתוֹ הַגָּג. אֲבָל בְּגָג אַחֵר אָסוּר. בְּשֶׁאֵינוֹ שָׁוֶה. אֲבָל אִם הָיָה שָׁוֶה מוּתָּר. תַּנֵּי. הָיָה שָׁם חַלּוֹן מַשְׁחִילִין דֶּרֶךְ חַלּוֹן.


**HALAKHAH:** “One lowers produce through a skylight on the holiday,” etc. The Mishnah is about this roof, but from another roof it is forbidden if it is not equal³. But if it was equal it is permitted. It was stated⁴: If there was a window, one lowers⁵ through the window.

footnotes:
³ All roofs of contiguous houses of equal height are considered as one roof. But if the roof of some house is of a different height, moving the produce from one roof to the other would be seen as workday exertion and is rabbinically forbidden on the holiday. Contradicted in Babli 36a.
⁴ Contradicted in Tosephta 4:3
⁵ משחילין is the correct original form of משילין.


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 5:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:1:3)


רַב יִרְמְיָה בְשֵׁם רַב. פּוֹרְסִין מַחֲצֶלֶת עַל גַּבֵּי שַׁיפוּף שֶׁל לְבֵינִים בַּשַּׁבָּת.


⁶4, Notes 38–46. Rav Jeremiah in the name of Rav: One spreads a mat over rows of bricks on the Sabbath⁷ items may not be moved, but they may be touched. It does not seem that this is the Yerushalmi’s attitude, as expressed by Rav in the question of the hunters. Nevertheless here the bricks are not touched; people sit on the mat. Babli 36a, *Šabbat*43a. In the reading of the Babli this is a Tannaitic statement and the bricks are not there for a future building project but are leftovers from a finished one..

footnotes:
⁶ The following text is copied from here in Šabbat
⁷ Even though the bricks are there as building materials one may turn them into seats if needed since they are not moved. In the Babli’s theory, muqṣeh


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 5:1:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:1:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:1:4)


אָמַר רִבִּי שִׁמְעוֹן בֵּירִבִּי יַנַּאי. אֲנִי לֹא שָׁמַעְתִּי מֵאַבָּא. אֲחוֹתִי אָמְרָה לִי מִשְּׁמוֹ. בֵּיצָה שֶׁנּוֹלְדָה בְיוֹם טוֹב סוֹמְכִין לָהּ כְּלִי בִּשְׁבִיל שֶׁלֹּא תִּתְגַּלְגֵּל. אֲבָל אֵין כּוֹפִין עָלֶיהָ כְלִי. וּשְׁמוּאֵל אָמַר. כּוֹפִין עָלֶיהָ כְלִי. אָמַר רִבִּי מָנָא. וּבִלְבַד שֶׁלֹּא יְהֵא כְלִי נוֹגֵעַ בְּגוּפָהּ שֶׁלְּבֵיצָה.


Rebbi Simeon ben Rebbi Yannai said: I did not hear from my father; my sister told me in his name. An egg which was laid on a holiday one props up against a vessel so it should not roll off. But one does not cover it with a vessel⁸. But Samuel says, one may cover it with a vessel. Rebbi Mana said, only if the vessel not touch the body of the egg⁹.

footnotes:
⁸ He holds that a vessel may be moved only for something that itself may be moved. Since the egg was not laid before the holiday it is not prepared and may not be moved.
⁹ Samuel holds that a vessel may be moved for any legitimate purpose. Babli Šabbat 42b. R. Mana requires that the vessel be moved in a way that certainly avoids moving the egg.


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 5:1:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:1:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:1:5)


תַּנֵּי רִבִּי הוֹשַׁעְיָה. נָחִיל שֶׁלִּדְבוֹרִים פּוֹרְסִין עָלֶיהָ סַדִּין. בַּחַמָּה מִפְּנֵי הַחַמָּה. וּבַגְּשָׁמִים מִפְּנֵי הַגְּשָׁמִים. מַה כְּרַב. כִּשְׁמוּאֵל. כָּאן מִלְּמַעֲלָן וְכָאן מִלְּמַטָּן.


Rebbi Hoshaia stated: One may spread a sheet over a swarm of bees¹⁰43a. in the summer because of the sun, in the rainy season because of the rain. Is this following Rav? Following Samuel? Here on top, there below¹¹.

footnotes:
¹⁰ The real reason naturally is to catch the bees when they are swarming on a Sabbath or holiday. If it can be done in a way which also protects the bees it may be done; Babli Šabbat
¹¹ Since Rav permits to cover the bricks he also will permit to cover the bees. But since Samuel prohibits touching the egg he might forbid here since the cloth necessarily will touch bees. Samuel also will agree here since the swarm is hanging on the branch of a tree, not lying on the ground and supported by it as in the case of the egg.


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 5:1:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:1:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:1:6)


רִבִּי בִּיסְנָא בְשֵׁם רִבִּי יוֹסֵה בֶּן חֲנִינָה. חֲפֵי לְסוֹטוֹת אָסוּר לְטַלְטְלָן. מְצַדְתְּא הַוְויָין פְרִיסִין וְהַוְיָין מִשְׁתַּרְפָן גַּו שִׁמְשָׁא. אַתּוֹן שָׁאֲלוּן לְרַב. מָהוּ מְטַלְטַלְתּוֹן. אֲמַר לוֹן. [אָסוּר לִיגַּע בְּהוֹן.] חַשְׁבִין עֲלֵיהוֹן מִתְנַנִּין תְּוֹחְתֵּי רֵאשֵׁיכוֹן וְשָׁרִי לְכוֹן מְטַלְטָלְּתוֹן.


Rebbi Bisna in the name of Rebbi Yose bar Ḥanina: It is forbidden to move the weaver’s beam weaving fine cloth¹² Chapter 6 appears לסוטה as Aramaic translation of Hebrew רְדִיד, a fancy outer garment of women. The identification of פחי as “weaver’s beam” follows a suggestion of Kohut (*Arukh completum s.v*. 2 פחה), accepted by Liebermann, that this is Arabic חפה.. The hunters were spreading traps and these were damaged by the sun. They came to ask Rav, may one move them? He told them, [it is forbidden to touch them.]¹³ Intend to put them under your heads and it is permitted for you to move them¹⁴.

footnotes:
¹² The translation is tentative. In Is.
¹³ Corrector’s addition.
¹⁴ If the traps are actually used as a support for the head they may be moved even though at nightfall this intended use is only in the owner’s mind.


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 5:1:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:1:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:1:7)


תַּנֵּי. חָבִית שֶׁלְטֵבֵל שֶׁנִּתְרוֹעֳעָה. מֶבִיא כְּלִי אַחֵר וְנוֹתְנוֹ תַחְתֶּיהָ. נִתְמַלֵּא. אָסוּר לְטַלְטְלוֹ. אָמַר רִבִּי זְעוּרָה. זֹאת אוֹמֶרֶת שֶׁעוֹשִׂין תַּקָּנָה לְמוּקְצֶה לְהַצִּילוֹ שֶׁלֹּא כְּדַרְכּוֹ. וְהָא תַנֵּי. עַל הָעֲבֹט שֶׁלְעֲנָבִים. עַל הַמַּעֲטָן שְׁלְזֵיתִים [שֶׁמְּשָׁכָן.] לֹא יִטּוֹל מֵהֶן בְּיוֹם טוֹב. וְאֵין צוֹרֶךְ לוֹמַר בַּשַּׁבָּת. אָמַר רַב חִסְדָּא. אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁזֶּה טֵבֵל וְזֵה טֵבֵל. זֶה יֵשׁ לוֹ מוּקְצֶה וְזֶה אֵין לוֹ מוּקְצֶה. הָדָא אָמְרָה. שֶׁעוֹשִׂין תַּקָּנָה לְמוּקְצֶה לְהַצִּילוֹ שְׁלֹּא כְדַרְכּוֹ. [תַּנֵּי.] נֵר שֶׁכָּבָה מַצִּילִין אֶת שַׁמְנוֹ. אָמַר רִבִּי יַעֲקֹב בַּר אָחָא. דְּרִבִּי שִׁמְעוֹן הִיא. דְּרִבִּי שִׁמְעוֹן אָמַר. כָּבָה. מוּתָּר לְטַלְטְלוֹ. אָמַר רִבִּי שְׁמוּאֵל בַּר רַב יִצְחָק. לֹא אָמַר אֶלָּא לְטַלְטְלוֹ. הָא לְהַצִּיל מַצִּילִין.


It was stated: If an amphora of *ṭevel*¹⁵ is produce subject to heave and tithe from which heave had not been taken. Since eating untithed produce is sinful, on a holiday *ṭevel* is *muqseh*. Since the produce is stored in an amphora, one speaks of wine or oil. became defective one brings another vessel and puts it under it. If it is filled, it is forbidden to move it¹⁶ produce.. Rebbi Ze`ira said, this implies that one makes an uncommon arrangement¹⁷ for *muqseh* to save it. But did we not state, from the vat of grapes, from the load of olives [which drew fluid] one may not take on the holiday, and is it not necessary to say on the Sabbath¹⁸ in all cases.)? Rav Ḥisda said, even though both of them are *tevel*, this is *muqṣeh* and that is not *muqṣeh*¹⁹ without question. In the case of *ṭevel* the produce is forbidden and it is forbidden to tithe on Sabbath and holiday. But this is a purely rabbinic prohibition; if tithe is given against the rules the produce is unquestionably food prepared for the holiday and permitted for consumption.. The following implies that one makes an uncommon arrangement for *muqseh* to save it. [It was stated.] If a light burned out, one saves its oil²⁰. Rebbi Jacob bar Aḥa said, it is Rebbi Simeon’s since Rebbi Simeon said, if it burned out it is permitted to move it²¹3:6, Tosephta *Šabbat*3:13. Since for R. Simeon there is no *muqseh*, the quote proves nothing.. Rebbi Samuel ben Rav Isaac said, he mentioned only “to move it,” therefore one is permitted to save it²².

footnotes:
¹⁵ Ṭevel
¹⁶ Since now the vessel is filled with muqseh
¹⁷ One proceeds in a way which is not what one would have done on a working day, where one would have brought another amphora and poured the entire contents into the new vessel.
¹⁸ A vat with newly harvested grapes or a vessel with olives whose cover was loaded down with weights in order to start extracting the oil from the olives. If the grapes were harvested to make wine, any fluid in the vat oozing out of grapes will be loaded into the wine press. In both cases, the fluids are not intended for consumption on the holiday and therefore not “prepared”. (On a Sabbath, fluid oozing out of a fruit is muqseh
¹⁹ In the case of vat and olive vessel the fluid is muqseh
²⁰ Even though while the light was burning the oil was not available for other uses since extinguishing a light is a biblical violation.
²¹ Mishnah Šabbat
²² Since Mishnah and Tosephta were formulated to show the difference between R. Simeon and the anonymous Tanna (presumed to be R. Meïr), the restriction to moving in the sources shows that R. Jacob bar Aḥa’s argument is not conclusive and the quote may be used to support R. Ze`ira,


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 5:1:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:1:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:1:8)


תַּנֵּי. נוֹתְנִין כְּלִי תַּחַת הַדֶּלֶף בַּשַּׁבָּת. נִתְמַלֵּא. שׁוֹפֵךְ וְשׁוֹנֶה וְאֵינוֹ חוֹשֵׁשׁ. אָמַר רִבִּי חֲנַנְיָה. עָשׂוּ אוֹתוֹ כִּנְהָרוֹת וּמַעְיָינוֹת שְׁבָּאוּ מחוּץ לַתְּחוּם לְתוֹךְ הַתְּחוּם. וְתַנֵּי כֵן. נְהָרוֹת וּמַעְיָינוֹת שֶׁבָּאוּ מִחוּץ הַתְּחוּם לַתְּחוּם. מְמַלֵּא מֵהֶן בַּשַּׂבָּת וְאֵין צָרִיךְ לוֹמַר בִּיוֹם טוֹב.


It was stated²³3:11.: “One puts a vessel under a leak on the Sabbath. If it was filled, one pours out and repeats without hesitation.” Rebbi Ḥanania said, they made this like rivers and sources coming from outside the Sabbath domain, as it was stated: from rivers and sources coming from outside the Sabbath domain one fills on the Sabbath and it is not necessary to say on a holiday²⁴121b, *Eruvin*46a, 48a. The language of the Babli sources states that these waters are “according to everybody’s feet”, see Mishnah 3..

footnotes:
²³ Babli 36b, Tosephta Šabbat
²⁴ Cf. Babli 39a, Šabbat



### Halakhah 2

###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 5:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:2:1)


**משנה:** כֹּל שֶׁהוּא מִשּׁוּם שְׁבוּת מִשּׁוּם רָשׁוּת מִשּׁוּם מִצְוָה בַּשַּׁבָּת חַיָיבִין עָלָיו בְּיוֹם טוֹב. אֵילּוּ הֵן מִשּׁוּם שְׁבוּת לֹא עוֹלִין בָּאִילָן וְלֹא רוֹכְבִין עַל גַּבֵּי בְּהֵמָה וְלֹא שָׁטִין עַל פְּנֵי הַמַּיִם וְלֹא מְסַפְּקִין וְלֹא מְטַפְּחִין וְלֹא מְרַקְּדִין. אֵילּוּ הֵן מִשּׁוּם רָשׁוּת לֹא דָנִין לֹא מְקַדְּשִׁין לֹא חוֹלְצִין וְלֹא מְיַיבְּמִין. וְאֵילּוּ הֵן מִשּׁוּם מִצְוָה לֹא מַקְדִּישִׁין וְלֹא מַעֲרִיכִין וְלֹא מַחֲרִימִין וְלֹא מַגְבִּיהִין תְּרוּמָה וּמַעַשְׂרוֹת בְּיוֹם טוֹב. כָּל אֵילּוּ בְּיוֹם טוֹב אָמְרוּ קַל וָחוֹמֶר לַשַׁבָּת. אֵין בֵּין יוֹם טוֹב לַשַּׁבָּת אֶלָּא אוֹכֶל נֶפֶשׁ בִּלְבָד:


**MISHNAH:** One is liable on the holiday for everything classified as Sabbath rest, classified as obligatory, classified as meritorious²⁵5, p. 96–99). Those classified as meritorious are deeds to the Temple. Most of the items are discussed in detail in the Halakhah. The reasons for the prohibitions given in the Babli cannot be presumed for the Yerushalmi.. These are classified as Sabbath rest: One does not climb on a tree, nor ride on an animal, nor sail on water, nor clap the hands in anger, nor clap the hands in joy, nor dance. These are classified as obligatory, one does not judge, nor contract preliminary marriage, nor give *ḥaliṣah,* nor enter levirate marriage. These are classified as meritorious, one does not dedicate²⁶, nor dedicate one’s worth²⁷ 27:2–8 and Tractate *Arakhin*., nor dedicate as *herem*²⁸. 18:14., nor collect heaves and tithes. All these were said regarding a holiday, so much more regarding the Sabbath. The only difference between holiday and Sabbath is preparation of food²⁹1:5..

footnotes:
²⁵ All the prohibitions listed in the Mishnah are rabbinic; therefore the expression “one is liable” is difficult to understand since usually it is reserved for severe biblical infractions. The activities classified as Sabbath rest are rabbinic restrictions, mostly to avoid possible biblical infractions. Those classified as obligatory may also be meritorious, nevertheless they are rabbinically forbidden on the holiday (cf. S. Liebermann, Tarbiz
²⁶ Animals as sacrifices or property to be given to the Temple.
²⁷ As described in Lev.
²⁸ Gifts to the priests, Num
²⁹ Repeated in Mishnah Megillah


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 5:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:2:2)


**הלכה:** שְׁבוּת שֶׁהִיא רָשׁוּת וְרָשׁוּת שֶׁהִיא מִצְוָה. אֵי זוֹ הִיא שְׁבוּת שֶׁהִיא רָשׁוּת. לֹא עוֹלִין בָּאִילָן וְלֹא רוֹכְבִין עַל גַּבֵּי בְּהֵמָה וְלֹא שָׁטִין עַל פְּנֵי הַמַּיִם [וְלֹא מְסַפְּקִין וְלֹא מְטַפְּחִין וְלֹא מְרַקְּדִין.] אֵי זוֹ הִיא רָשׁוּת שֶׁהִיא מִצְוָה. לֹא דָנִין וְלֹא מְקַדְּשִׁין לֹא חוֹלְצִין וְלֹא מְיַיבְּמִין.


**HALAKHAH:** Sabbath rest which is voluntary, and obligation which is meritorious. What is Sabbath rest which is voluntary? One does not climb on a tree, nor rides on an animal, nor sail on water, [nor clap his hands in anger, nor clap his hands in joy, nor dance.]³⁰ What is obligation which is meritorious? One does not judge, nor contract preliminary marriage, nor give *ḥaliṣah,* nor enter levirate marriage³¹.

footnotes:
³⁰ Corrector’s addition.
³¹ The paragraph makes clear that in the Mishnah רשות means “acts which may be obligatory and in that case are meritorious”, as discussed in detail by Liebermann (l. c. Note 25).


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 5:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:2:3)


תַּנֵּי. אֵין עוֹלִין בָּאִילָן בֵּין לַח בֵּין יָבֵשׁ. נִיחָא לַח. יָבֵשׁ. כָּךְ אָנוּ אוֹמְרִין. אָסוּר לָדוּשׁ עַל גַּבֵּי זְמוֹרָה בַשַּׁבַּת. אֶלָּא כְרִבִּי מֵאִיר. דְּרִבִּי מֵאִיר אָמַר. גֶּפֶן שֶׁיָּבְשָׁה אֲסוּרָה וְאֵינָהּ מְקַדֶּשֶׁת. דִּבְרֵי הַכֹּל. אָסְרוּ אִילָן יָבֵשׁ מִפְּנֵי אִילָן לַח. רַב חִיָיה בַּר אַשִּׁי אֲתַא וּפְרִי קוֹמֵי רַב. אֲמַר לֵיהּ. בֵיידָא [אֲ]תוּתֵהּ. אֲמַר לֵיהּ. [בְהַהִיא] תַּמַּרְתָּא עָקִימְתָא. אֲמַר לֵיהּ. מָאן שָׁרָא לָךְ.


It was stated: One does not climb on a tree whether moist³² or dried-up³³100b.. One understands moist; dried-up? So we are saying, it is forbidden to step on a twig on the Sabbath³⁴? Or does it follow Rebbi Meïr, as Rebbi Meïr said³⁵7:2., “a dried-up vine is forbidden but does not sanctify”³⁶? It is everybody’s opinion; they forbade the dried-up tree because of a moist tree. Rav Ḥiyya bar Ashi came running before Rav. He asked him, how did you come? He told him, [by] the warped date palm³⁷. He said to him, who permitted you?

footnotes:
³² Fruit bearing.
³³ Including trees without fruits. Babli Eruvin
³⁴ Even dry twigs; this is a minority opinion.
³⁵ Mishnah Kilaim
³⁶ It is forbidden to grow vegetables in a vineyard. This applies also to dry vines, but in contrast to active vines the vegetables do not become forbidden for usufruct.
³⁷ Implying that he stepped over the trunk, which for Rav is forbidden.


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 5:2:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:2:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:2:4)


רִבִּי אָחָא בְשֵׁם רַב. אָסוּר לָדוּשׁ עַל גַּבֵּי זְמוֹרָה בַשַּׁבָּת. הִיא שׁוֹרְשֵׁי אִילָן הִיא קוּלְסֵי אַכְרוּב. בִּגְבוֹהִין שְׁלֹשָׁה. אֲבָל אִם אֵינָן גְּבוֹהִין שְׁלֹשָׁה כְּאָרֶץ הֵם.


Rebbi aḥa³⁸10 (Note 116) and the name is supplied from there. in the name of Rav: It is forbidden to trample on a twig on the Sabbath. There is no difference between the roots of a tree or the leaves of cabbage. If they are three {hand-breadths} high³⁹. But if they are not three {hand-breadths} high they are like the ground.

footnotes:
³⁸ The name is missing here. The entire paragraph is copied from Eruvin
³⁹ Any growth three hand-breadths high is a tree for the laws of Sabbath and holiday.


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 5:2:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:2:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:2:5)


מִפְּנֵי מָה אָמְרוּ אֵין עוֹלִין בָּאִילָן. שְׁמָּא יִשְׁכַּח וְיֹאכַל אוֹ שֶׁמָּא יִשְׁכַּח ִוְיַרְעִיד. נִישְׁמְעִינָהּ מִן הָדָא. הָיָה רוֹכֵב עַל גַּבֵּי בְהֵמָה אוֹמְרִים לוֹ. רֵד. הָדָא אָמְרָה. שֶׁמָּא יִשְׁכַּח וְיַרְעִיד.


Why did they say that one does not climb on a tree? Is it lest he forget and eat, or maybe lest he forget and shake it? Let us hear from the following: If he was riding on an animal one tells him, descend. This implies, lest he forget and shake it⁴⁰.

footnotes:
⁴⁰ It will be shown in the sequel that riding on an animal is a biblical infraction. Since riding on an animal is quoted in the same sentence as climbing on a tree, the prohibition of climbing must be to avoid a biblical infraction. Shaking a tree to have its fruit fall down is the regular way of harvesting olives; therefore for all trees shaking is harvesting and a biblical transgression.


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 5:2:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:2:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:2:6)


מִפְּנֵי מָה אָמְרוּ. הָיָה רוֹכֵב עַל גַּבֵּי בְהֵמָה אוֹמְרִים לוֹ. רֵד. חֲבֵרַיָיא אָמְרֵי. שֶׁמָּא (תִּיטַק, תִינּוֹק) [תִינָּזֵק] הַבְּהֵמָה. אָמַר לוֹן רִבִּי יוֹסֵי. הַגַּע עַצְמָךְ שֶׁהָיָה הוֹגֵן אֶחָד גָּדוֹל. תַּנָּא רִבִּי אָחָא בַּר פַּפָּא קוֹמֵי רִבִּי זְעוּרָה. שַׁנְיָיא הִיא. שֶׁהוּא מְצוּוֶה עַל שְׁבִיתַת בְּהֵמָה כָמוֹהוּ. לְמַעַן יָנוּחַ שׁוֹרְךָ וַחֲמוֹרֶךָ. כָּמוֹךָ. אִית תַּנָּיֵי תַנֵּי. נִשְׁעַנִּים בַּבְּהֵמָה. אִית תַּנָּיֵי תַנֵּי. אֵין נִשְׁעַנִּים בַּבְּהֵמָה. אָמַר רַב חִסְדָּא. מָאן דְּאָמַר. נִשְׁעַנִּין. בְּבָרִיא. מָאן דְּאָמַר. אֵין נִשְׁעַנִּין. בְּתָשׁ. רִבִּי יוֹסֵה אוֹמֵר. אִיתְפַּלְּגוֹן רִבִּי בָּא בַּר מָמָל וַחֲבֵרַיָיא. חַד אָמַר. נִשְׁעַנִּים. וְחוֹרָנָה אָמַר. אֵין נִשְׁעַנִּים. מָאן דְּאָמַר. נִשְׁעַנִּים. בָּהוּא דְמִיסְתַּמֵּיךְ צִיבְחַד. מָאן דְּאָמַר. אֵין נִשְׁעַנִּין. בַּכּוֹבֵשׁ אֶת כְּבֵדוֹ. וְלָא יָדְעִין מָאן אֲמַר דָּא וּמָאן אֲמַר דָּא. מִן מַה דְלָא אֲמַר רִבִּי יוֹסֵה בְשֵׁם רִבִּי בָּא בַּר מָמָל כְּלוּם. הֲוֵי דוּ אֲמַר. נִשְׁעַנִּים בְּבָרִיא וְאֵין נִשְׁעַנִּים בְּתָשׁ.


Why did they say, if he was riding on an animal one tells him, descend? The colleagues say, maybe the animal will (be girded, be nursing) [be damaged]⁴¹. Rebbi Yose said to them, think of it, if it was a large camel! Rebbi Aḥa bar Pappus stated before Rebbi Ze`ira: There is a difference, because he is commanded about the rest of his animals like his own: *That your ox and your donkey rest*⁴² 23:12.*; like you*⁴³. 5:14.. There are Tannaim who state, one may lean on an animal. There are Tannaim who state, one may not lean on an animal. Rav Ḥisda said, he who said one may lean on, if he is healthy; he who said one may not lean on, if he is weak⁴⁴. Rebbi Yose says, Rebbi Abba bar Mamal and the colleagues disagreed. One said, one may lean on, but the other said, one may not lean on. He who said one may lean on, if he leans a little bit; he who said one may not lean on, if he puts on his full weight. We do not know who said what. Since Rebbi Yose did not say anything in the name of Rebbi Abba bar Mamal, this implies⁴⁵155a, *Ḥagigah*16b) any leaning on animals is forbidden. that a healthy person may lean on, a weak one may not lean on.

footnotes:
⁴¹ The text is not clear, nor is its meaning. The scribe’s text is תיטק but the letter ט is damaged and was read as נו by the Venice printer and נז by the corrector. There is no Hebrew root נטק (or יטק) on record; the Arabic root נטק means “to gird”. In the Babli 36b the reason is purely rabbinic, lest one cut a twig from a tree to drive the animal. The reader’s own conjecture as to the meaning will be as good as any other proposed interpretation.
⁴² Ex.
⁴³ Deut
⁴⁴ A healthy person leans on an animal as he would lean on a human; this must be permitted. A sickly person might use the animal to support his entire weight; this is counted as a biblical infraction.
⁴⁵ Since R. Yose reported the disagreement between Amoraim without further discussion he indicated that this disagreement was unimportant since the apparent disagreement between Tannaim already was explained away by Rav Ḥisda; the latter’s explanation was accepted also in the Galilean Academy. In the Babli (Šabbat


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 5:2:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:2:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:2:7)


וְלֹא שָׁטִין עַל פְּנֵי הַמַּיִם. רִבִּי יַעֲקֹב בַּר זַבְדִּי בְשֵׁם רִבִּי אַבָּהוּ. זֹאת אוֹמֶרֶת. שֶׁאָסוּר לְהַשִּׁיט דָּבָר וּלְהוֹלִיכוֹ מֵאֶצְלוֹ. וְדִכְוָותָהּ. אָסוּר לְאָדָם לְהַשִּׁיט דָּבָר וּלְהָבִיאוֹ אֶצְלוֹ. רִבִּי אַבָּא מָרִי וְרִבִּי מַתַּנְיָה הֲווֹן יְתִיבִין. חָמוּן לְחַד בַּר נַשׁ פַּצֵּל מַיָּא לְכָא וּלְכָא וְשָׁתִי. אָמַר רִבִּי אַבָּא מָרִי לְרִבִּי מַתַּנְיָה. הָדָא הִיא דְאָמַר רִבִּי יַעֲקֹב בַּר זַבְדִּי בְשֵׁם רִבִּי אַבָּהוּ. אָסוּר לְאָדָם לְהַשִּׁיט דָּבָר וּלְהוֹלִיכוֹ מֵאֶצְלוֹ. אָמַר לֵיהּ. הָדָא הִיא דְתַנִּינָן. שׁוֹבֵר אָדָם אֶת הֶחָבִית לוֹכַל מִמֶּנָּה גְרוֹגְרוֹת.


“Nor sail on water⁴⁶” Rebbi Jacob bar Zavdi in the name of Rebbi Abbahu: This implies that it is forbidden to set something under sail to send it away from him. And similarly, it is forbidden to set something under sail to bring it to him. Rebbi Abba Mari and Rebbi Mattaniah were sitting⁴⁷. They saw a person splitting the water⁴⁶ to both sides and drinking. Rebbi Abba Mari said to Rebbi Mattaniah, this is what Rebbi Jacob bar Zavdi in the name of Rebbi Abbahu, a person is forbidden to set something under sail to send it away from him⁴⁸. He answered, it is what we have stated, “a person may break open an amphora to eat dried figs from it⁴⁹”

footnotes:
⁴⁶ The person sailing does not do anything; the wind provides the power which pushes the water away on both sides of the boat.
⁴⁷ On a weekday; therefore they did not have to actually intervene.
⁴⁶ The person sailing does not do anything; the wind provides the power which pushes the water away on both sides of the boat.
⁴⁸ Since the sailboat splits the water and the man observed did split the water.
⁴⁹ If the amphora is tightly closed with clay that sticks to its walls, one may open it by damaging the vessel even though the intent is for a positive action. Therefore one certainly may split the water in order to drink.


###### Jerusalem Talmud Beitzah 5:2:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:2:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Jerusalem%20Talmud%20Beitzah/r/5:2:8)


וְלֹא מְסַפְּקִין. סִיפּוּק שֶׁהוּא מֵחַמָּתוֹ. כְּמַה דְאַתְּ אָמַר וַיִּחַר אַף בָּלָק אֶל בִּלְעָם וַיִּסְפֹּק אֶת כַּפָּיו.


“Nor clap the hands in anger.” סִיפּוּק is in anger, as you are saying, *Balaq got angry about Bileam and he clapped his hands in anger*⁵⁰. 24:10..
